=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There is an integer sequence S of length N. Initially, all elements of S are 0.
You are also given two integer sequences of length Q: P=(P_1,P_2,\dots,P_Q) and V=(V_1,V_2,\dots,V_Q).
Snuke wants to perform Q operations on the sequence S in order. The i-th operation is as follows:

- Perform one of the following:
- Replace each of the elements S_1, S_2, \dots, S_{P_i} with V_i. However, before this operation, if there is an element among S_1, S_2, \dots, S_{P_i} that is strictly greater than V_i, Snuke will start crying.
- Replace each of the elements S_{P_i}, S_{P_i+1}, \dots, S_N with V_i. However, before this operation, if there is an element among S_{P_i}, S_{P_i+1}, \dots, S_N that is strictly greater than V_i, Snuke will start crying.



Find the number of sequences of Q operations where Snuke can perform all operations without crying, modulo 998244353.
Two sequences of operations are distinguished if and only if there is 1 \leq i \leq Q such that the choice for the i-th operation is different.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N Q
P_1 V_1
P_2 V_2
\vdots
P_Q V_Q

Output

Print the answer as an integer.

Constraints


- 2 \leq N \leq 5000
- 1 \leq Q \leq 5000
- 1 \leq P_i \leq N
- 1 \leq V_i \leq 10^9
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

8 3
1 8
8 1
2 1

Sample Output 1

1

Snuke can perform the three operations without crying as follows:

- Replace S_1 with 8.
- Replace S_8 with 1.
- Replace S_2, S_3, \dots, S_8 with 1.

No other sequences of operations satisfy the conditions, so the answer is 1. For example, if he replaces S_1, S_2, \dots, S_8 with 8 in the first operation, he will cry in the second operation regardless of the choice.

Sample Input 2

8 3
8 1
1 8
1 2

Sample Output 2

0

No matter how he performs the first two operations, he will cry in the third operation.

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Pseudo:

import sys
MOD=998244353
def main():
    data=list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data: return
    N=data[0]; Q=data[1]
    P=[0]*Q; V=[0]*Q
    idx=2
    for i in range(Q):
        P[i]=data[idx]; V[i]=data[idx+1]; idx+=2
    color=[0]*Q # 1 L, 2 R
    for i in range(Q):
        vi=V[i]; pi=P[i]
        for j in range(i+1,Q):
            if vi > V[j]:
                if pi == P[j]:
                    print(0); return
                if pi < P[j]:
                    # L,R
                    if color[i]==2 or color[j]==1: print(0); return
                    color[i]=1; color[j]=2
                else: # pi > P[j]
                    if color[i]==1 or color[j]==2: print(0); return
                    color[i]=2; color[j]=1
    free=color.count(0)
    print(pow(2, free, MOD))

Let's still attempt to prove pair condition in more detail to be comfortable.

Define for each operation i, interval I_i. Suppose sequence valid. Take any position x. Let T_x be list of operation indices whose I_i contains x, in increasing order. At the time of the k-th operation in T_x, the value at x just before is the value from the (k-1)-th operation (because no other operation covering x in between). Since operation is valid, V_{prev} <= V_{current}. Thus V values along T_x are nondecreasing. Therefore for any i<j both in T_x, V_i <= V_j. Hence if V_i>V_j, no position can be in both I_i and I_j, i.e., intervals disjoint. Conversely, if for every pair i<j with V_i>V_j intervals are disjoint, then for any position x, any two covering operations i<j must have V_i<=V_j (otherwise they'd be a decreasing pair with overlapping x). Thus T_x values nondecreasing, so each operation valid. Actually need if all decreasing pairs disjoint, then any overlapping pair has V_i<=V_j. Then for any position x, covering sequence values nondecreasing. So valid. Thus validity iff every inversion pair's intervals are disjoint. This is even cleaner. Then count colorings where for each inversion pair intervals disjoint. For each inversion pair, determine which color choices make intervals disjoint. Let's enumerate:
- Same colors always overlap -> not disjoint.
- L_i,R_j: intervals [1,P_i] and [P_j,N] disjoint iff P_i < P_j (since need P_j > P_i). If P_i < P_j, this is the only disjoint choice? R_i,L_j: [P_i,N] and [1,P_j] disjoint iff P_i > P_j. So if P_i<P_j, only L_i,R_j. If P_i>P_j, only R_i,L_j. If equal, none. This matches.

Thus constraints are exactly "for each inversion, the only allowed color pair". No constraints for non-inversions because if intervals overlap, V_i<=V_j so valid; if disjoint, valid. So count assignments satisfying all these forced pairs. This is solid.

def solve():
    import sys
    MOD=998244353
    it = iter(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N = next(it); Q = next(it)
    P = [0]*Q; V=[0]*Q
    for i in range(Q):
        P[i]=next(it); V[i]=next(it)
    color=[0]*Q
    for i in range(Q):
        vi=V[i]; pi=P[i]
        for j in range(i+1,Q):
            if vi > V[j]:
                pj=P[j]
                if pi == pj:
                    print(0); return
                if pi < pj:
                    if color[i] == 2 or color[j] == 1:
                        print(0); return
                    color[i]=1; color[j]=2
                else:
                    if color[i] == 1 or color[j] == 2:
                        print(0); return
                    color[i]=2; color[j]=1
    free=0
    for c in color:
        if c==0: free+=1
    print(pow(2, free, MOD))

Now, let's revisit the proof of "if V_i > V_j, intervals must be disjoint" in terms of operation validity. Suppose intervals overlap at x. At time j, current S_x is from the latest operation before j that covers x. If there is an operation h between i and j covering x with V_h <= V_j, then current S_x could be <=V_j even though V_i>V_j. But then consider operation h: before h, current S_x is from latest covering before h, which could be i if no covering between i and h, or some other. If V_h <= V_j < V_i, and if i is latest covering before h, then h would be invalid. But what if there is another covering between i and h with V > V_i? That would be even larger; h invalid. If there is a covering between i and h with V <= V_h, then sequence has decrease from V_i to that? Let's formalize: If i and j both cover x and V_i > V_j, consider the sequence of operations covering x from i to j. Let k be the first operation after i in this sequence. At time k, the current value at x is V_i (since no covering between i and k). For k to be valid, need V_i <= V_k. Thus V_k >= V_i > V_j. Then by induction, the values of covering operations from i onward are nondecreasing, so the last before j has value >= V_i > V_j, making j invalid. This proves necessity without relying on all pairs? Wait, what if k is j itself? Then V_i <= V_j false, invalid. If there are intermediate operations, the first after i must have V_k >= V_i > V_j. Then the sequence from k to j also must have nondecreasing? At least the last before j has value >= V_i > V_j. Let's prove by induction: Let t be the last operation covering x before j (or i if none). Since every consecutive covering pair a<b must satisfy V_a <= V_b (otherwise b invalid), the values from i to t are nondecreasing, so V_t >= V_i > V_j. Then j invalid. Thus necessity of nondecreasing covering sequence. Good.

Sufficiency: If for every inversion pair intervals disjoint, then for any position x, any two covering operations i<j cannot have V_i>V_j, so V_i<=V_j. Thus covering sequence nondecreasing. Then each operation valid because for any x in its interval, the previous covering value <= current V. Good.

Now, let's maybe derive a more direct condition for conflict to ensure no missed constraints due to transitive closure of unit clauses. Unit clauses are just variable assignments. If no variable assigned both values, consistent. There is no clause like (x=L) OR (y=R). So yes.

Let's see if pair constraints could be represented as implications? For inversion pair i<j with P_i<P_j, allowed only (L,R). This is not a clause but a conjunction of two unit assignments. If we think of 2-SAT, it's (x_i=L) AND (x_j=R). Yes.

Now, let's consider if there are constraints from non-inversion pairs that could be violated by arbitrary completion if intervals overlap? Non-inversion means V_i<=V_j. If intervals overlap, current value at overlap before j might be from some operation h with V_h > V_j, even though V_i<=V_j. Wait! This is important. Our condition "for every inversion pair intervals disjoint" ensures no pair i<j with V_i>V_j overlaps. But what about a non-inversion pair i<j with V_i<=V_j, but there is an intermediate operation h covering the same position with V_h > V_j, and h is not in an inversion with i? Actually h<j and V_h>V_j would be an inversion pair (h,j) if h covers x. Then intervals h and j overlap at x, so our condition would require h,j disjoint, contradiction. Thus if all inversion pairs disjoint, no such h can cover x. More generally, for any position x, consider the sequence of covering operations. If there were a decrease V_a > V_b for consecutive covering operations a<b, then pair a,b is an inversion and intervals overlap at x, violating disjointness. Thus no decrease. So valid. Good.

Let's write code:

import sys
MOD = 998244353

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N = data[0]
    Q = data[1]
    P = [0]*Q
    V = [0]*Q
    idx = 2
    for i in range(Q):
        P[i] = data[idx]
        V[i] = data[idx+1]
        idx += 2

    # suffix minimum of V
    suf = [10**30]*(Q+1)
    for i in range(Q-1, -1, -1):
        v = V[i]
        nxt = suf[i+1]
        suf[i] = v if v < nxt else nxt

    color = [0]*Q  # 0: unknown, 1: prefix(L), 2: suffix(R)
    for i in range(Q):
        vi = V[i]
        if vi <= suf[i+1]:
            continue
        pi = P[i]
        for j in range(i+1, Q):
            if vi > V[j]:
                pj = P[j]
                if pi == pj:
                    print(0)
                    return
                if pi < pj:
                    # must be L_i, R_j
                    if color[i] == 2 or color[j] == 1:
                        print(0)
                        return
                    color[i] = 1
                    color[j] = 2
                else:
                    # must be R_i, L_j
                    if color[i] == 1 or color[j] == 2:
                        print(0)
                        return
                    color[i] = 2
                    color[j] = 1

    free = 0
    for c in color:
        if c == 0:
            free += 1
    print(pow(2, free, MOD))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Let's test small: V=[5,1,4,2,3]. i=0 vi5>suf, loop all 4. i=1 vi1<=suf skip. i=2 vi4>suf, loop j=3,4 (2). i=3 vi2<=suf? suf[4]=3, vi2<=3 skip. i=4 skip. Total ~6. Not O(Q^2). To have all i have later smaller, V must be decreasing? Not necessarily: V=[5,4,1,3,2]? i=0 vi5>suf loop4; i=1 vi4>suf? later min1, loop3; i=2 vi1 skip; i=3 vi3>suf? later min2, loop1. Total ~8. O(Q^2) maybe if sequence has many "descents" but no conflict? V=[5,4,3,2,1] conflict early. But if conflict not early? Let's see V=[5,1,4,3,2]? i=0 loop4; i=1 skip; i=2 vi4>suf loop2; i=3 vi3>suf loop1. Total 7. O(Q^2) still if Q large with many local maxima. Could be ~Q^2/4. 12.5M max anyway. Fine.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "suf" skip and "vi > V[j]" check; if vi <= suf[i+1], skip, so not all pairs. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "color.count(0)" if color is list of ints; okay.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a hidden issue with "P_i" and "V_i" arrays if input lines have extra spaces; split handles.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N" not used and maybe if N is large but Q small; no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "MOD" if answer is negative? no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "free" count
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

MOD = 998244353

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return

    N = data[0]
    Q = data[1]

    P = [0] * Q
    V = [0] * Q
    idx = 2
    for i in range(Q):
        P[i] = data[idx]
        V[i] = data[idx + 1]
        idx += 2

    # suffix minimum of V
    suf = [10**30] * (Q + 1)
    for i in range(Q - 1, -1, -1):
        v = V[i]
        nxt = suf[i + 1]
        suf[i] = v if v < nxt else nxt

    # color: 0 = unknown, 1 = prefix(L), 2 = suffix(R)
    color = [0] * Q

    for i in range(Q):
        vi = V[i]
        if vi <= suf[i + 1]:
            continue

        pi = P[i]
        ci = color[i]

        for j in range(i + 1, Q):
            if vi > V[j]:
                pj = P[j]

                if pi == pj:
                    print(0)
                    return

                if pi < pj:
                    # must be L_i, R_j
                    if ci == 2 or color[j] == 1:
                        print(0)
                        return
                    ci = 1
                    color[j] = 2
                else:
                    # must be R_i, L_j
                    if ci == 1 or color[j] == 2:
                        print(0)
                        return
                    ci = 2
                    color[j] = 1

        color[i] = ci

    free = color.count(0)
    print(pow(2, free, MOD))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
